To the Metanet Staff: If this could be pinned, I would really appreciate it. Thanks in advance!

Before I registered for the forums, I was checking out the Echo Pack and came across a quote by spiker5 that I liked:
Quote
Yea, the rate these are going, there might even be, bum bum bum, noob level packs!!!!
Even though my NUMA member number is a bit over 9000, I don't think I'm a noob, but I know I can improve. That's why I'm posting the Novus Series, a whole column of levels, which I tried to make as episodic as I could. The first 9 episodes are just normal levels, but the last episode has a surprise theme for you all! I'd really appreciate some feedback and criticism from experienced mapmakers. I look forward to my stay on the Metanet Forums, and I hope my prescence will make others enjoy my stay here too! Now that my introduction is over, I give you (without further ado)... The Novus Series! Enjoy!

~ Pheidippides ~

#225200 89 2006-09-07T16:24:12Z Pheidippides
I have an idea for a contest, similar to chasethebase's N League, but different. It would be a highscoring competition, structured like Major League Baseball (Only in America!). You see, there would be two main Leagues (the Mare and Raigan Leagues), sort of like the American and National Leagues in Baseball. From there we'd have two divisions within each League (for now I'm just calling them Divisions 1 and 2). Highscorers play against each other 1-on-1 on certain maps, and player's Win-Loss Record will help me rank him/her within their Division. After enough games have been played, we'll start the playoffs. The person with the best record from each division will get in, so we'll have semifinals and then finals. In the semifinals, the winners of Divisions 1 and 2 will play each other within their respective Leagues (D1 & D2 from Mare play each other and D1 & D2 from Raigan play each other). The winners of these games play each other in the World Series of N!

What do you guys think?
#225383 91 2006-09-07T21:37:12Z Pheidippides
It's not complicated when you see it visually. Go to http://mlb.mlb.com/NASApp/mlb/mlb/standings/index.jsp and maybe my idea will make a little more sense. The table would look like that, with everyone having a Win-Loss Record that allowed them to be sorted by who has the most wins. The leaders of their respective divisions make the playoffs, which then becomes like a ladder tournament. I hope that link helps.

#12418 1 2006-11-23T02:49:06Z Pheidippides
::: FAQ :::

Q: What is a doppelgnger?
A: A doppelgnger is the ghostly in some cases, the physical double of a living person.

Q: What do doppelgngers have to do with N?
A: If doppelgngers didn't have anything to do with N, why would I start a Doppelgnger Contest? You'll find out soon enough... Let's lay down the rules.

::: Rules :::

1. Mapmakers will work in pairs to create two maps, which will comprise one entry.
2. To make these two maps, the mapmakers will make a tileset. The first mapmaker will make a map using that tileset, and the second mapmaker will make a map using the same tileset, only inverted (All tiles become open space, all open space becomes tiles).
3. Entries will be submitted in one .txt file with both levels in userlevels format ($name#author#Doppleganger#code).
4. Each map will be judged separately out of 20 according to the rubric.
5. The scores for each map will be added together for a total score out of 40.
6. Although this would be an unlikely occurence, mapmakers may not use old maps.

::: Scoring Rubric :::

*/10 ~ Enjoyment
*/10 ~ Aesthetics

::: Prizes :::

First Place, Second Place, and Third Place ~ Signature Tag
Fourth Place and on ~ A pat on the back.

The final results are attached to this post. Thank you for competing!
Attachment: post-21-1166814679.txt

Hey Everyone,

Sept and yahoozy stole my idea! I was going to post some of my work tonight. Their pack probably blows mine out of the water, but here it goes:

I was planning on using these levels for something else, but I felt that it would be better for me to release them like this. On a vacation a few weeks ago, I made about four or five maps during my down-time. I had one or two maps in storage on my flash drive, and decided to put them together and make a bit of a mini-pack. Inspired by my travels and the week that followed my trip are ten non-episodic maps for your enjoyment. I know it isnt a lot of maps, but in my opinion they are worth sharing with you guys. Enjoy!

Where will you be at Twilight ?

~ Pheidippides

#316894 17 2007-04-29T23:52:20Z Pheidippides
Don't the ratings round up at 4.75? Not all of those votes on any of those maps might be fives. For example, if you had just one 5 and a 4.5 that would round up to 5. If you had 40 votes, 4 of those could be 4.5s and you'd still have a 5 rating.

Edit: Hendor, nevermore wouldn't have to snipe you (I'm not implying he did, we're just being hypothetical.) All he would have to do is give the 4.5 that brings the average rating below 4.75. Just because any rating goes from supposedly all 5s to a 4.5 doesn't mean it was sniped.

#330439 18 2007-06-12T17:08:48Z Pheidippides
I'd have to go with what nevermore said about being creative. I really only make maps when I feel creative enough to do it. Sometimes I get on a hot streak, but other times (like now) I'm pretty cold. Never force yourself to make a map. When you get an idea, that's the time to make maps. Just take your idea, dump it into Ned, and tweak it until you like it.

This may sound slightly hypocritical, because I do use a lot of the mainstream techniques that Zres and usaswim mentioned, but that's part of my creative process. I often get inspiration from other author's maps, then take a spin on them that's totally my own. Unfortunately, I really can't completely disregard looks, but that's one thing I'm working on. I've got some maps stored on my flash drive that are pretty out there, at least for me. But I'll never be like nevermore and yahoozy, who can totally focus on gameplay.

Anyways, you've gotta find out how you make maps, see what works and what doesn't, and then work at improving the points that you don't like in your maps. I know I playtest my maps until I can all-gold them, and have fun doing it. That (and a bit of aesthetics) are my criteria for my maps. What are your criteria?

I hope this helps somewhat. It probably won't, but that's because I suck at these lecture-type advice-giving deals. [/ramble ramble ramble]

We need to set down some uniform tags that people can use to aid organization and information. So far, we've got some interesting ideas.

#16226 1 2007-07-14T14:20:12Z Pheidippides
Well, this pack was actually supposed to be my farewell. It was supposed to have more maps, too. Instead SUBLiME kept me here, and I regained my passion for mapmaking and my interest in the goings-on of this community. So, having realized I wasnt about to walk away from it all, I continued the pack with a different idea in mind. I just made maps not as a way of saying goodbye, but just for the sake of mapmaking.

Because I kept my focus for these maps away from NUMA (as I did with Monologue, this packs predecessor), I did not just make your average maps I made fantastic maps. Indeed, many maps in this pack are among the best Ive ever made. Just as with Monologue, I came away from this pack feeling like a far better mapmaker than I was when I started. Let me tell you, its a great feeling. And to think all I had to do was stop submitting my maps to NUMA and just enjoy mapmaking.

What else brought about such a change? I took my time. In fact, I started this pack in April or May. Aside from the odd NUMA map for the MNNM League or something, Ive spent the last 3 or 4 months on these 20 maps. Each map took me a good majority of the day to finish, mostly because I didnt stop playing and tweaking a map until I could all-gold it (Sometimes I didnt even stop there.). I made maybe 2 maps a week at the very most; some weeks I made no maps. It was taking a very long time by just about any standard, which is mainly why I cut back on the number of maps in the pack. Still, I give you a respectable 20 maps, each of which was crafted delicately and playtested to perfection. Ladies and gentlemen, Dialogue.

#17079 1 2007-09-04T01:35:31Z Pheidippides
So I was on a long car ride this weekend, listening to some of the tunes on my MP3 player, and I thought that mapmakers are kind of like musical artists. Don't ask me how, I couldn't tell you. So that got me thinking, "Musicians have concerts. Mapmakers don't really have much that's remotely 'live'. Can that be changed?"

I thought so, and I remembered the IRC. I don't use the IRC myself, but I know it's pretty big in this community because people can communicate instantly. So then I had this idea. What if some mapmakers got on the IRC and put on a "concert", I suppose, for the others gathered on the channel? They would make maps for the crowd, providing the night's entertainment for the rest of the IRC-goers. These would be brand new maps, prepared possibly in advance, but maybe not - who knows?

So basically, a few mapmakers organize a time, inform the rest of the community, and presto! We have a somewhat novel way of releasing maps to the public. I'm not sure this is very different from what might go on on the IRC already. As I said, I don't use it. This is just some idea I had on a boring car ride, and I thought I'd share. What do you think?

#17392 1 2007-10-01T23:50:43Z Pheidippides

Twilight has arrived! I don't think I could describe this pack much better than epigone already has:

Quote: epigone, 2007-09-24T17:46:40Z
Many of you probably forgot all about the whisperings about this map pack. In fact, we deliberately kept it a secret to build more mystique. Little did we know how many problems, setbacks, and various other delays would arise. This project was started almost a year ago; originally meant to be 100 maps, four people each making five episodes, it has been reduced to 80, and only 3 members. They are epigone, Pheidippides, and Zres. The fourth was Hawkk, in case you didn't know.

In a way, playing this pack is going to be a look back into the past. Some of the maps are going to feel retro, especially with how our respective styles have grown and evolved since the majority of the maps have been made. There is some new stuff in addition to the older maps, and all of the maps have been edited at least once over the last couple of months to update them. Think of the older maps as a fine wine, getting better with age.

Getting better with age, indeed. We certainly have spent a lot of time working on this pack, and I must say it's been a lot of fun to work with epigone and Zres. They both deserve a big hand for their hard work. I feel confident saying that our hard work is greatly reflected in these maps, and it really has made us better mapmakers than we were when we started.

When you play these maps, think of them as an extended column instead of a column and 6 episodes. That's the way the difficulty curves (Or at least should curve.) In case you're curious, here's a list of who made which maps:

Episode 00: Pheidippides
Episode 01: epigone
Episode 02: Zres
Episode 03: Pheidippides
Episode 04: Zres
Episode 05: Pheidippides
Episode 06: epigone
Episode 07: Zres
Episode 08: epigone
Episode 09: Pheidippides
Episode 10: Zres
Episode 11: epigone
Episode 12: Pheidippides
Episode 13: epigone

Here's where it gets interesting:

14-0: Pheidippides
14-1: Zres
14-2: Zres
14-3: Pheidippides & epigone
14-4: Zres
15-0: Pheidippides
15-1: Pheidippides & epigone
15-2: epigone
15-3: Pheidippides
15-4: Pheidippides

And there you have it! Do you know where you are? Because Twilight is upon us. Enjoy it!

#369245 12 2007-10-03T03:14:46Z Pheidippides
Congrats. As you will find, there is still much to be done...

If you're the creative type, you should try making maps and visit NUMA.
If you want to continue playing N, you could download levelpacks to beat or try out Unreality's new program. Maybe try highscoring.
You could also do what many forumers have done, which is stop playing N all together and just chill here with some cool people. Make images, debate, or do whatever else suits you.

Pick one, two, or all three! I'll admit though, it's hard to be good at both mapmaking and highscoring. One of the two doesn't come as naturally as the other for most people. There are exceptions, like tktktk and George, and even L3X, crappitrash, Hendor, and zapkt have made a few maps, but you don't see people like them often.

Enjoy your stay.

#17562 1 2007-10-15T21:54:04Z Pheidippides
My favorite part of The Sunday N was probably Artist Commentary of the Third Kind. I love hearing what authors have to say about their own maps. In Issue 22 of The Sunday N, KinGAleX described the then-new section thus:
Quote: KinGAleX
The general premise is for an author to take ten of their maps (from NUMA or level packs) and provide a short explanation of say, why they like the map, a cool story behind the map (or anything to do with the map): basically whatever they find most interesting about these maps.
I found this idea really cool, and now that it seems The Sunday N has faded away, I cant let it die. Thats why Im encouraging you to post new ACotTK articles here. The rules remain the same: Pick ten (10) of your maps and write a little bit about each one. However, I do have some guidelines regarding this thread:

Ten maps means ten maps. I know some of you have a lot of maps that you want to talk about, but for the sake of variety, Im going to have to ask you to limit the number of maps you post here. Dont think you can talk about ten maps and then double-post with another ten maps. I want to mix up the authors in the thread, so please do not post more than one ACotTK article every seven (7) days.

Spelling and grammar are important. I want this thread to be as readable as The Sunday N was. Please, do your best as far as spelling and grammar are concerned.

When posting an article, please follow this format:
Quote: Sample ACotTK Article
Artist Commentary of the Third Kind, by <username>

<Name of first map>: Map description.
<Name of second map>: Map description.

Et cetera.

Attach a .txt file to your post with the level data for your ten maps in userlevels format: $Title#Author#ACotTK#Data#. Note that the Map Type is ACotTK.

If these guidelines are met, it will be easy (and hopefully fun ) to continue an organized Artist Commentary of the Third Kind. And dont think the old ACotTK articles will fade away with The Sunday N, either. I am working on an archive for all maps and their authors articles from The Sunday N. My progress is attached to this post. If you want to help, by all means go right ahead. Note: If you want to help, it would be best if you had Microsoft Office 2007.

And now, Artist Commentary of the Third Kind for all! I'll be posting my article soon.

Edit: Reworded some things so I don't sound like an all-important jerk. Maybe now this topic will be a bit more inviting. Still, I do stand by my guidelines. As with The Sunday N, organization and readability are a factor here. Enjoy?

#375184 7 2007-10-29T01:44:30Z Pheidippides
/me bumps thread with my own article.

Artist Commentary of the Third Kind, by Pheidippides

Airborn Inferno: This is arguably the toughest map Ive ever made, and while it can be frustrating at times, I find it incredibly addictive. Its also surprisingly fast-paced if youve played it as much as I have. Looking back, there are things I would change about the map, but I still love playing it the way I originally made it.

Dysprosis: I just had to mention this one. This one was inspired by maximos Flight, which is probably my favorite map of all time. Everything just works so well here, I love it. Period.

Fragile Psyche: This was my first good race and my favorite race map of mine. Thorne, which I made right after Fragile Psyche, may have been bitesized, but I prefer this one. I like the flow of it more, and I personally think its more creative than Thorne. Weird, I demoed them both in 1288 frames.

Latent Heat: I think this map started my transcendence to the next level of mapmaking. After this map (and McP3000s failed Dynasty), my maps simply became much better. Also, I really enjoy the whole midair minefield aspect that the one-ways create.

Manifest Destiny: This is arguably one of my best maps from Twilight, and one of my personal favorites from the pack. Actually, it was only the second map I made for the project. And Im glad I made it that early Manifest Destiny really set the tone for my Twilight maps. As with Latent Heat, I feel that this map was another step up for my mapmaking.

Nonconformist: I wouldnt have made this map if it werent for Yahoozy. It was his idea to listen to an entire album and only that album while mapmaking. He posted about it on the forums, and I gave it a try. This map was a product of Gutterflower by the Goo Goo Dolls, one of my favorite albums. It only makes sense, then, that this is one of my favorite maps of mine. Order and chaos blended so well in the tileset, and I love the gameplay.

Shards of Twilight, Traverse: I wanted to talk about a map from Monologue, but its really hard to decide between these two. They were definitely the standouts of the pack. I love the tileset on Shards of Twilight, and the jumper-esque gameplay is choice. The gauss turrets work really well. As for Traverse, anything astheoceansblue calls so damn solid is definitely worth a mention. I love the chaingun up top, too.

Subverse, August and Everything After, Fallout Complex: I think I should talk about these next three together. I was really pursuing a certain (heavily-astheoceansblue-inspired) style in these three, beginning with Subverse. That was easily my favorite map from Dialogue, and one of the few maps where Ive used a laser drone really well. I had a few interesting ideas in August and Everything After, some of which I employed in Fallout Complex. To me, Fallout Complex is really the perfection of the style in Subverse, and Im immensely satisfied with it.

#17837 1 2007-11-07T22:38:53Z Pheidippides
Transcend. (Tentative Title), by Pheidippides

The Idea: This is a level pack with a storyline but not just any storyline. Nope, this is in no way like the little creative blurb you might see in a maps description on NUMA. Were talking a story. That means heroes, villains, plot twists, conflict, everything. My goal here is to create a pack with something enjoyable besides the maps in it. I think its going to be a lot of fun, but I suppose thats ultimately for you to decide.

The Current Story: Story 01 // The Awakening
Spoiler: Stage 01 // Grandeur CityIt certainly had been a while since N had last seen Grandeur City. Still, as he began to glimpse the thriving metropolis on the horizon, he slowed his pace from a run to a casual stroll, taking in the countryside. After spending months atop Mount Doom in the hopes of achieving transcendence, he could identify with the isolation of this locale and wanted to enjoy his last few minutes in it before plunging back into life in the city.

As he walked, he felt a certain lightness in his step he hadnt noticed before, as if through achieving transcendence he had lifted a weight off his entire body. He felt more alert, more focused, and began to wonder if transcendence had physical as well as spiritual implications for the ninjas who took the time to achieve it. Its a shame so few ninjas achieve transcendence anymore. he thought as he approached the gleaming mass of industry ahead. Indeed, it seemed that ninjas simply couldnt be bothered with the difficulties of achieving transcendence anymore, and had given up on it altogether.

Drawing closer to the city line, N began to see drones lazily coasting down the streets, floorguards zipping along on the expressways, and ninjas hurriedly running and jumping to their various destinations, and he remembered how he had missed Grandeur City. His training on Mount Doom had been intense, but it lacked a regularity he had so long depended upon. Once again his daily life would be scheduled and predictable, or so he thought, because all at once the unscheduled and unpredictable had happened.

Chaingun drones slowly glided toward Grandeur City, opening fire and sweeping the streets with their bullets. N felt himself break out of his casual pace into a full sprint for the city, dodging round after round of chaingun fire as he went. Whats happening? N thought as he continued at breakneck speed. Suddenly he remembered, as if he had been there just yesterday, that deep within the city there was a shelter where he and the other city-goers would be safe.

Ive got to get to that shelter and figure out how to stop this. N reasoned. Shaken but determined, N ran as fast as he could for the heart of Grandeur City. [Map Link]
Spoiler: Stage 02 // Underground EscapeAfter a heart-pounding race through the city, N found the shelter he had so frantically sought. As he closed in on it, he could see that he was not the only one who had remembered the sanctuary. A small crowd of ninjas was already gathered inside, and as N entered they greeted him, though with urgent undertones in their voices. Amongst the group was a purple ninja, apparently the leader, who introduced herself as Q. "Hey, blue, ya got a name?" she asked as N pushed his way through the crowd.

"N," he replied.

"Another one? It seems like youre all named N. Anytime somebody names a ninja, they always call him N. Not too many creative parents around these days, are there?"

"Not too many creative writers, more like."

More and more ninjas continued to swarm to the shelter, chaingun drones gliding along in their wake. Noticing that the drones were too close for comfort, Q called for a lockdown, and the shelters steel gate began to close. At seeing the descent of the gate many ninjas nearby quickened their pace, hoping to get to safety before it was too late. One of them, a young green ninja, tripped and fell in the mad dash. To N, it seemed to happen in slow motion, and he was suddenly aware of a flashback taking hold of his senses
***
It was ten years ago, the last time that Grandeur City had been invaded. N was sprinting alongside his little sister, M, trying to reach the same shelter where he now stood comfortably in safety. The steel gate had begun to close, and N felt his mind suddenly swimming in panic. A chaingun drone rounded a corner nearby, and while it wasnt between N and M and the steel safe haven ahead, it had a clear shot at the pair as they ran by. In dodging the hail of bullets, N felt Ms hand lip out of his grasp. Horror-stricken, he turned to watch his little sister trip and stumble to the ground. With the gate just a few feet from closing completely, N had a choice to make save M and forfeit their chance at shelter, or save himself.

The young N chose to save himself and dove beneath the closing gate. From inside the shelter, he heard a chaingun drone preparing to fire
***
N awoke from his trance to notice that no time had passed in the world currently around him. He found that young green ninja in the same mortal peril as he was before, in the same mortal peril as M had been in all those years ago. Though his mistake then could never be undone, N was determined not to make the same mistake this time. Pushing his way through the crowd he lunged into the open, where chaingun drones were sure to be arriving at any moment.

Once outside, the shelters steadily closing steel door seemed to move unnervingly quickly, but N gathered himself and ran as hard as he could to where the green ninja lay, trembling and awaiting the eerie whirr of a chaingun drone loading its next round. In a swift move N scooped up the green ninja and turned around, and suddenly something of a lifeless grey in the distance caught his eye and that fateful whirring noise reached his ear. N now ran faster than ever towards his ever-shrinking window of opportunity at the shelters door and slid underneath the mass of steel just in time, with the green ninja in tow. He heard another round of chaingun fire pounding against the other side of the door, a mere second or two from hitting its mark.

Inside the shelter, N was cheered by his fellow ninjas for his act of heroism. The green ninja, whose name was G, was in awe at his blue savior. Q quickly ended the congratulations by calling N over to her, and G followed in his wake.

"Impressive," Q said as she patted N on the back, "very impressive. Your little buddy here is lucky that you were around. Not a single other ninja in this shelter wouldve done what you just did, N. That makes me think Im pretty lucky to have you around here, too. You see, I could really use your help in settling the score with Doctor I."

"Dr. Who?" N questioned.

"Dr. I. Hes the one who was behind the attacks ten years ago, and Im sure he was behind the attacks today. That means hes out of hiding and hes got a plan, which means bad news. Id go after him again, but I lost my entire team in trying to chase him away the last time. Thats where you come in. I need skilled ninjas like you to help me."

"I just achieved transcendence, so I think Ive got the skill youre looking for. After Mother Thwumping Impossible, taking down this Dr. I should be a cakewalk," N replied confidently.

"Good. That makes two of us who have achieved transcendence," Q reasoned. "With a few more able-bodied ninjas, well be ready for Dr. I."

A small voice chimed in, "Hey! I may not be transcended, but I want in on this!" It was G. "Its the least I could do for N to lend you guys a hand. Please!

"You?" Q said skeptically. "Havent you had enough danger for a while, kid?"

"Aw, cmon! Ill be alright. Who knows? You might be thankful in the end," G claimed desperately.

"To be honest, with your nagging Im already not so sure Im thankful N saved you," Q joked. "Actually, it looks like youre a good fit for this group. To be volunteering for something like this just to repay N shows both guts and loyalty. Welcome aboard, G."

"Thank you, Q!" said a delighted G. "I wont let you down!"

Out of the crowd came a red ninja, who strode up to Q and said, "From what I just heard, youre looking for transcended ninjas to help you stop these attacks. Id like to get even with those drones myself. T, a transcended ninja, at your service."

Where were you when G was in danger out there? Q thought. To Ts offer, she replied, "Sure thing, T. The blue one here is N, and the little guy is G. Welcome."

"Okay, now there are four of us," N remarked. "That should be enough. How do we go about stopping Dr. I, now?" He asked.

"By following me," said Q, and she ducked into another room in the shelter. Moving a desk in the middle of the room, she revealed a hole in the floor with a ladder descending into it. She clambered down the hole, and the others followed. Upon reaching the bottom, the group stepped off into what looked like an underground aqueduct. Just then, the ground shook.

"Whats happening?" G asked, worried.

"That was probably from the attacks going on above ground," answered Q. "Now quit worrying and follow me; were getting out of this city." [Map Link]
Spoiler: Stage 03 // Fiery PlainsThe four ninjas were hurriedly scrambling through the aqueduct when the earth around them gave another quake. This tremor was much stronger, much closer to the group, than the first had been. Before anyone could say a word to question or explain it N, Q, G, and T felt their escape route flying upward from underground. The piece of aqueduct they had been running in had been blown into the air by a rockets blast, and out of the ends of the pipe the four could see debris all around them and a rocky plain below.

"Were not even in Grandeur City anymore!" Q shouted above the roar of the explosion. "What could possess Dr. Is army to attack a random location so far away from their intended target?"

"Who knows?" N yelled back. "All I know is that we cant stay here, or by the time we find out itll be too late. Look around. We can jump down using all that falling debris. Follow my lead."

With that, N led the escapees out into the midst of the blast and began to descend towards the jagged landscape below. [Map Link]
Spoiler: Stage 04 // Ancient ChasmNearing the ground, N began to think about what would happen when he, Q, G, and T finally touched down in the middle of the rocky plain. There were rockets flying all around, and few places to hide. There were a few shallow craters here and there, but those would only provide very temporary shelter, if any at all, for the group. As he bounded around on the falling debris, he noticed the ancient Ruins of Nopolis.

They were the remains of an ancient ninja city, and had been preserved but left well alone. No one wanted to enter the Ruins, especially after hearing the widespread ghost stories of ancient curses and clever traps. The place had baffled historians for years. Needless to say, N wasnt so enthused about the idea of going there himself. Thankfully, there was a large chance he wouldnt have to hide there if the rocket launchers saw him enter, theyd turn the ancient buildings into dust without a seconds hesitation.

That still left N with the problem of finding somewhere where he and the other three could hide. He was hesitating on his descent now, peering around for shelter. Theyd hit the ground soon, and theyd have to hit it running. G, who for the only ninja in the group who wasnt transcended was doing a fantastic job of keeping pace with N, noticed the leaders uneasiness.

"Do we have a plan, N?" he asked.

"Nope, not a thing," N replied, frustrated.

Looking a ways off from the Ruins, G spotted a small opening in the ground. It was well protected by the surrounding rocks and looked pretty deep, deep enough at least to shelter the four ninjas from rocket fire until the army gave up the attack and left the plain for the city. As they got closer to the ground, G still couldnt see any bottom to the gap in the rock. Could it be a cave? He wondered. Then he called aloud, "Hey, N! See that hole over there?" He gestured to the opening.

"Yeah, I see it. Its probably just another crater," N answered. "We wont be able to stay there too long. Otherwise the rockets could call for help and theyd have us cornered. Keep looking for a hiding place, G."

"Keep looking? I think this deserves a second look, N. It actually looks like a cave."

After a moment of exasperation, N gave in to Gs request and took another look at the gap in the rocks. G was right it was a cave. That was the ideal place to hide. Even if the rocket launchers signaled for other drones to look for them inside the cave, there was probably a huge network of caves beneath the rocky ground. Finding the group would be impossible in such a vast place. His mind made up, N leapt to the ground and ran for the entrance to the cave. G, Q, and T followed.

Suddenly a rocket hit the ground just in front N, and the smoke and dust from the blast obscured his view of the mouth of the cave. The four came to a stop amidst the flurry of rocket fire, trying to glimpse their intended refuge. Another rocket whizzed past them, and they dodged it just in time. It sailed away into the sky, and the rush of wind it created as it flew by disturbed the smoky haze for just an instant. An instant was all N needed. Regaining sight of the cave he motioned to the others to follow him and leapt into its entrance, leaving the chaos of the plain behind him.

All four ninjas made it safely inside, and upon looking deeper into their sanctuary saw that it wasnt as safe a haven as they had hoped it would be. Jagged rocks jutted from the floor and ceiling of the cavern, impeding their progress into its depths. If they were to truly be safe, they needed to get farther inside, but N faltered. "I dont know how we can get through here, guys," he said. "This looks too dangerous to cross."

"I think I can help!" G interjected. "This is my kind of thing lots of tricks, like cornerkicks." He motioned to the tips of the rocks. "I could jump off those pretty easily. Want me to lead?"

"Well," N began, "I guess we have no choice. Im not much with cornerkicks and other gimmicks, so it looks like youre up, kid."

"I agree. Go ahead, G, get us started," Q added.

"Alright. Here goes nothing," G said, and with that he jumped out into the middle of the caves jagged floor below and began his trek across the dangerous and ancient chasm. [Map Link]
Spoiler: Stage 05 // Vaulted ChambersThanks to Gs amazing agility, the group had found a safe route across the deadly chasm in the cave floor. Quickly the four ninjas pressed onward, hoping to disappear from the sensors of the drones they knew would eventually pursue them. The darkness of the cave descended upon them with every step, ushering them into its depths. A voice spoke up, "This may not be such a good idea, guys. In trying to lose the drones, we might lose ourselves. No sense hiding where even we dont know where we are."

"Dont worry, T," Q said. "Youre transcended, for crying out loud! Youve got the ability to handle a little walk in the dark."

"Yeah," N added. "Wherever theres an opening in the cave, light will filter in. The darkness is only temporary, and well be rid of it when we find a way out."

"Maybe sooner than that," G said. "Look over there."

Light was emanating from somewhere in the distance, although G was right that it wasnt a way out. If it were an opening in the cave, the light would be a steady glow from the sunshine above. But the light wasnt steady or from above. It was flickering and dancing about the cave, like fire. Nearing it, N realized that it was, in fact, fire a torch. He rushed ahead of the group, grabbed it, and, wheeling around to face the other three, noticed that something was wrong. "Guys, wheres T?"

"What!?" replied G and Q together, and looking around they too saw that he was nowhere to be found.

Unsettled, N peered around and called out, "Hey, T!" Q and G echoed him, but their cries aroused no response from the lifeless cave walls. Further efforts to call T back to them yielded the same result. T had vanished.

"Looks like there are just three of us now," Q reasoned.

Her eerie message reverberated around the cavern and within the troubled thoughts of her comrades, surrounding them. N felt betrayed by the torch, which instead of dispelling the darkness had increased its weight, created fear rather than destroying it. Q called him back to his senses. "N, we should probably keep going."

"Right," he replied. "Follow me."

Continuing through the cavern, N began to see why the torch had been there. It had appeared out of place at the time, but as he scanned the walls of the cave he noticed writing. It looked like it had been scrawled there ages ago, and although N didnt understand the language, it made sense to him someone must have been down here before. "Guys, look!" He gestured to the wall.

"What do you suppose it says?" G wondered.

"Its not important," Q answered. "This writing means that someone was down here before, and that they must have come down here from somewhere. Look, theres more up ahead! If we follow this, we might find a way out."

"Exactly," N stated.

Following the mysterious cave writings, the group turned a corner and saw a light ahead. This time, it certainly wasnt fire. Steady light streamed from a hole in the ceiling of the cavern. The three reached the entrance and ascended, to Ns horror, into the Ruins of Nopolis. "No!" N cried. "Why here!?"

"Better up here than down there," G pointed out.

"Many would argue you on that, G, but I guess you have a point," N admitted. "This city may be falling down, but its organized. It shouldnt be hard to get out of here. We should split up and search for a way out of the city walls. Ill go this way."

The three went in separate directions and began their search for an exit from the mysterious ruins. Quickly N came upon a large tower, and an idea struck him. If he climbed the ruined buildings, he might be able to go over the walls and escape without having to find a way through them. Hopeful, N ran into the building and began his ascent. [Map Link]
Spoiler: Stage 06 // Sneak DistrictSplit up? Inside the Ruins of Nopolis? What was N thinking? Q thought to herself. After the group disbanded in search of a way out of the Ruins, she had begun to worry about G and now watched the young ninja from a distance, hoping to protect him from whatever dangers might be lurking in the ancient city. G was, after all, in the most danger because he was the least skilled and most reckless of the three ninjas, although N, in Qs opinion, was beginning to challenge him on the latter.

Q and her unsuspecting protg wandered the gloomy city streets, making their way towards the city walls. Their footsteps echoed as they walked. Q noticed that G had paused to look around, and so she did likewise, stopping just out of Gs sight. Immediately, Q realized something horrible the echo of their footsteps had not stopped. Despite the fact that both Q and G were standing still, step after step could be heard reverberating off the Ruins. They were not alone.

Now frantic, Q wheeled around and peered through the haze that hung over the streets, and the footsteps continued, growing louder. "G, over here!" she whispered urgently, and she came out of her hiding place. G looked around, appearing worried, and he spotted Q. He put his hand to his ear as if to ask Q if she heard the echoes, and Q nodded and motioned for him to be quiet.

The two were now both scanning their surroundings, trying to find the source of the echoing footsteps. The sounds pressed on methodically, frighteningly, growing louder with each passing minute. The haze severely limited their sight as they fruitlessly searched. Then Q heard something else amidst the horrible scene a second set of footsteps.

This set of footsteps was louder than the first, and their pace indicated that the culprit was clearly running. Q turned towards G, but she couldnt see him. The haze had become so thick that it appeared nearly solid, but just then a light wind had blown by, lifting the blinding fog. G was no longer where he had been standing, and Q suddenly knew who was behind the second set of footsteps. Whatever was making those noises must have gone after G! Q realized. Ive got to help him! Now in a worried frenzy, she ran off after the sound of Gs footsteps.

As she caught up to the echoes, she noticed that G was not in trouble. A shadowy figure ahead of him, however, was. G had spotted the owner of the footsteps and set off in pursuit. G looked over his shoulder and saw Q behind him. "Q!" He called back. "This guys heading for the walls! He must know a way out of the Ruins!"

Quickening her pace, Q chased after the shadowy figure and its young green pursuer. [Map Link]
Spoiler: Stage 07 // Sandstorm BlockadeQ and G relentlessly hounded the mysterious figure through the streets of the dilapidated city of Nopolis, and soon they found themselves closing in on their mark. Their prey had wandered into a dead end, with high walls looming all around. The chase was over. Curious to unmask the reckless runaway, the two ninjas slowly approached their helpless captive.

At least, they thought he was helpless. Just then, the trapped ninja made a single incredible leap to the top of the sheer walls. Q and G paused for a moment in amazement before resuming their pursuit, walljumping upwards as quickly as possible. Upon reaching the top, the pair breathed a collective sigh of relief, for standing between the runaway ninja and his escape was a familiar face N had joined the hunt.

But again, just when the chase seemed to be over, the ninjas realized that it had merely taken another turn. The strange ninja, peering from the edge of the wall to his captors and back again to the edge on which he stood, refused to be cornered. Without hesitation, he dove from the high city wall to the craggy plain below.

"Not the friendly type, is he?" N quipped as his eyes followed the ninja's fall. "Must've had a really good reason to avoid us like that. I wonder what he was so desperate to keep from us?"

"More like what he is so desperate to keep from us," said G as he looked down towards the ground. "I don't know how, but he survived that fall." He pointed to a figure in the distance, fleeing from the ruins. There was no mistaking him it was their escapee.

"We should keep tailing him," Q suggested. "If he's got that good a reason to keep running, I think we ought to find out what it is."

The others agreed, and together they descended onto the rocky plain, though not with the agility of the bizarre figure they were tailing. As they clambered across the rough landscape, however, they began to close in on the ninja once again, and N and Q picked up the pace as they neared their goal. G was right on their heels, but a sound reached the young ninja's ears that caused him to falter.

Beep.

Before he could utter a warning, G saw the plain erupt into a chaotic whirlwind of rock and dust. The blast was so great in magnitude that N and Q, who had not heard the nearly inaudible trigger, were lifted off their feet and sent sailing backwards. They landed quite some distance away, but G could see them stirring and knew that they would be all right, though a bit shaken up. The problem of the three's elusive prey, however, still remained, and G had a decision to make. He could give up the chase to avoid deserting his friends, or he could go it alone.

G chose to continue the chase, and leapt into the swirling sandstorm. [Map Link]
Spoiler: Stage 08 // Lost GrandeurG emerged from the sandstorm and stared out onto the vast rocky expanse that still lay before him. To his dismay, there was no sign of the ninja he had been pursuing, or even of any other living thing, for what looked like miles. Then, in the corner of his eye, G spotted a flash of color as it moved stealthily between two rocks. Seeing no reason to remain idle, he darted towards the source of the blur, and immediately regretted the decision.

In an instant G was seized by two rather large and strong ninjas who had also hidden amongst the rocks and craters that blanketed the plain. He struggled and squirmed as much as he could, but to no avail the strength of these brutes was unnatural. A voice emerged from the spot where G had seen the blur of movement moments before. "Always the reckless one, arent we?" it said smugly. "It appears the hunter has become the hunted."

The voice was cold, cruel, and eerily familiar. Horrified, G looked up to meet the gaze of his captor as he emerged from the shadows.
***
As the dust settled on the blast-ravaged plain, Q managed to clear her dizzy mind. A short distance away lay N, who was still in a stupor from the force of the explosion. Q gave him a shake to try to snap him out of it, but the blue ninja remained unconscious. "N!" she called, but no one answered except the echo of the lifeless rocks around her. Precious time was slipping away, widening the gap between N and Q and their comrade as he hurried on in pursuit of the mysterious ninja. Refusing to wait any longer, Q hoisted N off the ground and carried him onward.
***
The force of the blast had sent N careening into a boulder, and the world was vanishing in a whirl of color as he stumbled to the ground. Soon darkness overcame him, and he fell unconscious. From amidst the blackness that clouded his mind, N heard someone calling his name, and suddenly the darkness dissipated. N, however, did not awake from his stupor to the plain he knew he was lying on. Instead, he found himself in Grandeur City ten years ago. Standing amidst the chaos of the citys first invasion, N realized who had been calling him.

Looking back, N saw his little sister, M, lying frightened and helpless on the ground. Ahead was the shelter she had tried so desperately to reach, but the door was closing it was too late to rescue her and still reach safety. Nonetheless, N ignored his instinct of self-preservation and dashed out into the fray to redeem his grave mistake of all those years ago. Diving through a hail of chaingun fire, he reached M and lifted her to her feet. Her fearful expression became one of wonder and gratitude, but neither sister nor brother uttered a word. N was in awe at his sisters sweet, almost forgiving glance. With new hope, N and M sprinted off into the ruined city. [Map Link]
Spoiler: Stage 09 // Dizzy ForestN and M darted through the smoke and dust of the ruined city completely undisturbed. It was as if the chaingun drones didnt even see the pair as they fled to the rocky plain on the outskirts of the metropolis. M strangely seemed to know exactly where she was going, and even though N couldnt possibly imagine her destination, he followed her.

Together they crossed the plain and came towards a forest, the mighty trees of which loomed massively over them. They crept into the woods, and N noticed that there wasnt a sign of life anywhere. Confident that they were now safe, M turned to her older brother.

"Thanks for turning back," M said.

"I'm sorry," N replied. "I'm sorry I didnt the first time."

"What matters is that you did this time," she said reassuringly. "I forgive you, N. Theres no time for apologies now, anyway. Dr. Is planning something terrible, and his preparations are almost complete. Hes even captured G! You need to reach his hideout in the middle of this forest before its too late."

Her words echoed in Ns ears, and N began to lose focus. The world was once again disappearing in a blur of color, and in an instant M departed from him.
***
Hopelessly trapped in the ambush, G had had no choice but to surrender. From the plain, his captors had led him across the vast expanse of rocks and craters and into a forest, where after a while they came upon a small modern building hidden amongst the trees. There were drones on patrol.

When they came to the door, a voice prompted the group for identification. The leader stepped forward.

"T," he said coolly. The red ninja turned to his companions, and added, "And a special guest for Dr. I." The grunts laughed and gave G a hard jab in the back, shoving him through the doors.

Once inside, G realized that the building was not nearly as small as it had appeared. He stared down into a large laboratory, the floor of which was several yards below his ground-level vantage point. The whirr of computers and machinery filled the large room with a constant hum.

G saw a row of large tubes along the wall, and to his horror he saw ninjas inside them, suspended in some odd fluid and breathing through tubes. They appeared to be sleeping. A voice called from the floor.

"Well, T, have you done as you were ordered?" it said.

"Better, Doctor," T answered. "In fact, the green runt is here to see you. Hes a strong kid, but hasnt achieved transcendence yet a perfect candidate for testing your latest formula."

"Excellent," the doctor said as he ascended the stairs from the laboratory floor. He was a short, stocky, orange ninja wearing a pair of goggles on his forehead and a greedy smirk on his face. "We dont often find untranscended ninjas around here. Youre right, T, the Artificial Transcendence formula could turn him into a very useful protg."

"And if he would join us, hed be useful in perfecting more powerful formulas as well," T added. At this, G spoke up.

"Why would I join you?" he demanded. T turned to him with a look of mixed anger and exasperation.

"Why?" he replied. "Why? How do you think I pulled off those stunts out there, G? Sheer luck? No, those stunts were the work of Dr. I. Hes created a formula that alters a ninjas genetic code to push him beyond the limits even of transcendence. With this formula, Ive reached Protoscendence, and am twice as powerful as your friends N and Q. Your friends are doomed if they challenge us, and you will be too, G, if you defy me."

G paused. After a moment, he replied, "Then consider me doomed."

"You fool, G!" T shouted angrily. "Transcendence could have been yours! Unlimited power could have been yours! Now youll be lucky if you still have your life when our plans are complete."

"Take the runt to the cell blocks," Dr. I ordered. "Hell pay for his foolishness. Lock him up!"
***
Q reached the edge of the plain, still carrying N. She had reached a large forest, and ducked into the trees to hide herself and N from view. Tired, she set him down and stopped to rest awhile. In the silence, she heard a strange voice.

"Q," it called faintly. To Qs relief, she saw that it was N who had spoken, though he was still very weak.

"N, thank goodness youre awake," Q said. N seemed not to notice.

"Q, w-weve gotta k-keep going," the blue ninja stammered. "Gs in t-trouble. M-middle of the f-forest" His voice trailed off. After pausing for a moment, N stood up. He was still dizzy from the injury to his head, but nonetheless he staggered off into the forest. Unsure what had come over N but unable to let him wander into the forest alone, Q followed. [Map Link]

The Details: You can expect roughly 15 maps in a story. I'll try to release maps as often as I can but, as you will find, writing the story does get pretty involved, and that's on top of mapmaking. Hopefully this will all be worth the wait. Also, if you're wondering, the inspiration for this came from Shadow the Hedgehog and other Sonic games. I think you'll see a few similarities as I go along, but I'm going to try to be as original as I can. Enjoy.

The Blog: I've started a blog to archive the chapters of Transcend. as the story progresses. Right now it's just a more professional-looking version of what you see here, but in the future it may become more than that, so stay tuned. You'll find the blog here.

No, I never skipped it in practice mode, or any level in that manner. But I did rebeat it (with a time below 90.. above is impossible) and it fixed it.

I don't really get what george is saying though.. I mean, you start the first level of every episode at 90 seconds and it always records it, even if you have less time than you started with.

No, what George is saying is this:

Say you start 83-3 with, I don't know, 100 seconds. Say you finish with less than 10 seconds. That means that if you started with 90 seconds (like you would if you selected "Play Level 3" in the highscore menu), you would run out of time (taken longer than 90 seconds), and your particular demo would run on into negative time before actually completing the level. In a long level like 83-3, this is entirely possible. While you still completed the level in the context of the episode, you have to go faster to complete the level by itself in highscore mode, with a starting time of 90 seconds.
#379302 19 2007-11-14T03:43:48Z Pheidippides
First option. Use tags to find DDAs and N-arts. I agree with epigone about N being a game meant to be played. Actually, I'm not even sure I want races on the top rated amidst the actions and puzzles. To me, actions and puzzles are the core of N, and everything else is just, I don't know, extra. Aside from the few DDAs and races that are truly amazing and that stand above all the rest, DDAs and races just don't hit home for me like an action or puzzle would. N-arts are cool, but not essential like actions and puzzles are.

Basically, I look at it this way: Without actions and puzzles, N wouldn't be what it is. That's why those maps deserve to be recognized by themselves and by default. I understand races might be hard to eliminate from the other playables in this sense, but that should be okay anyway. Should anyone want to see the best of the "extra" (nonplayable) categories, all they have to do is go by tags. Simple.

#379316 3 2007-11-14T04:30:57Z Pheidippides
I haven't listened to a lot of "new" music recently, but I'll walk you through my playlist from before my cross country race this past Saturday.

Landslide by Fleetwood Mac
-- Great relaxing song here, calmed my nerves a bit on the bus ride to the course. I actually woke up to this song that morning, as it just happened to be playing on the radio when my alarm went off. After hearing it at 7am, I was in a great mood for the rest of the day. Good stuff.

Daylight Fading by Counting Crows
-- Love that first line, "Waiting for the moon to come and light me up inside..." Great tune, some nice guitar in there. I suggest giving it a listen.

The Space Between and What Would You Say by Dave Matthews Band
-- Classic. Great songs to keep you relaxed and energetic at the same time, which is just what I need on a race day.

First Time by Lifehouse
-- I think that's the name of the song. Anyway, really great tune.

Losing My Religion by R.E.M.
-- Another classic.

I can't remember the others I went through, but I finished it off with...

Luckie St. by Cartel.
-- That opening guitar stuff gets me pumped. I listened to this last so I'd be ready to go come the race. Aside from running a good race on Saturday, I really enjoyed the song. The cold in the song lyrics kind of fit with the chilly day of the race too. This is just some good music.

That's the best I've got for now.

#385566 8 2007-12-03T01:26:15Z Pheidippides
I think Atilla really puts this question in perspective. The only possible answer is that style is not exclusive, and while krusch, NIGHTMARE, yahoozy, atob, and others have very distinctive styles, the styles are not specifically theirs. Still, you should consider the disadvantages to "trying on" another author's style, such as the fact that most often you can't pull it off as well as they can. Plagiarism should never be a question if you're simply incorporating another author's style into your own maps because, as everyone's said, they're still your maps. Unoriginality? Maybe, if utilizing another author's style becomes a highly recurring theme in someone's maps. But never plagiarism.
#385570 8 2007-12-03T01:33:26Z Pheidippides
Now that's a great idea, mam. Although, and I'm sorta playing devil's advocate here, shortening the 90-second time limit tests the creativity of mapmakers. In other words, it takes skill to effectively press a player for time without altering the game's clock itself, and we'd miss out on that with the ability to alter the clock. In the sense of in-game episode play, I see no problems. Racing against the clock would be great in the actual game.
#385591 12 2007-12-03T02:25:43Z Pheidippides
Quote: Ditocoaf, December 02, 2007 09:08 pm
Someone help me out here-- I'm incredibly bad at recognizing any author's "style."  What makes a map have a certain style?  Do I have a style?  How would one imitate a style?
Look at maps by atob, yahoozy, and other well-known authors. There are recurring elements in their maps. These recurring elements, aside from being part of what makes their maps so well-liked, define their style. Basically, an author's style is the collection of all their mapmaking habits.

Edit: And basically, to answer your other question, this is a one-sided debate, because when you think about it there's no way style can be as exclusive as max_ride is implying that it is. A style defines an author, so using another author's style excessively can be seen as trying to be that author. This does make style a bit exclusive, but max_ride is really making a point that trying on different styles every once in a while isn't against any rule.
#385614 18 2007-12-03T03:36:37Z Pheidippides
Quote: Ditocoaf, December 02, 2007 09:57 pm
Looking at those (well actually yahoozy's link was broken) I began to see what you mean about "recurring elements," but I still don't see anything that I could actually recognize an author by, even if there were only, say, 10 mapmakers in all of NUMA. I don't think I have any sort of "style," and I still don't really understand the concept.

I'm not denying that there are author styles, but like I said, I'm just trying to comprehend the idea.

Sorry about the broken link . I think being able to recognize style and developing your own style will come in time. After all, you've only been here a month. As you browse NUMA, look for those recurring elements I talked about. The more you browse NUMA, the more easily you will be able to associate these recurring elements with particular authors. For example, when I see a map with a modern-looking, sort of "industrial" tileset coupled rockets and square-ish gold placement, I can be 99.9% sure that it's an astheoceansblue map.

As for the development of your own style, you need to look at what you do well when you're making maps, and try to utilize those techniques in your other maps. For me, I've found that big clumps of gold works in my maps as opposed to scattered single pieces or tiny groups of gold, and so I use that big-clump style of gold placement more often. Examples here, here, and here. It's all really hard to explain. Legacy Team, I think we need a Style Manifesto.

And no, max_ride, that's not too much to ask. That's been the opinion of pretty much everyone in this thread.

#394628 87 2007-12-30T02:04:44Z Pheidippides
Quote: spoon, December 28, 2007 04:54 am
Also, is there a guy who you go to for EV training?

Nah. EV training is work you have to put into the training of a Pokemon before it ever sees any kind of battle. In case you don't know the basics, Pokemon earn Effort Values (EVs) when they defeat other Pokemon. Each individual Pokemon is worth a specific amount of a certain type (HP, Atk, Def, etc.) of EVs. Each 4 EVs a Pokemon earns for a stat coincides with a one-point increase for that stat.

Pokemon can earn a maximum of 510 EVs total, so you have to spread them over certain stats wisely. Sure, you could put 255 on Atk and Speed for a physical sweeper, but you'd get the same effect with 252 in each stat because EVs work in groups of 4. So you can spend those last 6 EVs on another stat to get an extra point somewhere, like HP or Defense.

Calcium, Carbos, and the other stat-boosting items add 10 EVs to the stat they boost. However, you can only use 10 of a certain kind of item on a Pokemon. Pokerus and Macho Brace double the EVs a Pokemon earns in each battle. The berries that increase tameness but decrease a stat decrease the stat by 10 EVs.

Now let's consider my Ninetales. It has Pokerus and Macho Brace, so it earns 4 times the amount of EVs it would normally receive. I gave it 10 Calciums for Special Attack, and 10 Carboses for Speed. This puts it at 100 Special Attack EVs and 100 Speed EVs. I then proceeded to beat 38 Magikarps with my Vulpix fresh out of its egg, so I maxed out my Speed EVs at 252. I then went and beat 39 Gastly's (I miscounted.), so it's Special Attack EVs went up to 255. I had 3 EVs to spare, so I beat a Geodude to get a few Defense EVs. However, they don't do anything, because I only could get 3 of the EVs or I would go over 510. After 510 EVs, any extra EVs don't count.

For the record, Magikarp is worth 1 Speed EV, Gastly is worth 1 Special Attack EV, and Geodude is worth 1 Defense EV. My Pokerus/Macho Brace combo quadrupled Ninetales' EV income, so its training was completed a lot quicker. I suggest using http://pokemon.marriland.com/ to look up how many EVs each Pokemon is worth. That site is very useful for anything Pokemon, actually.

Note that EV training must be done fresh out of the egg/wild. Otherwise, you will earn EVs you may not want by battling before beginning EV training. I hope all of this helps.

#394866 2 2007-12-31T00:18:50Z Pheidippides
This involves either locked doors or, more preferably, NaN'ing the drone. You should read LV's Tutorial about it to learn more, but for this specific situation, here's what to do:

1) Immediately after you add the laser drone, go back to the debug menu.
2) Click inside the level data box, at the very end of the data.
3) Hit the L key to load the level without clicking out of the level data box.

This creates an error in the code that makes the drone stay put. LV goes into great detail about all sorts of code editing, and his tutorial even explains why this editing does what it does. You should definitely check it out, and good luck with those ideas.
#394890 6 2007-12-31T01:47:20Z Pheidippides
Quote: bhz, December 30, 2007 07:46 pm
Pheidippides - When I do what you said the laser drone moves. So I must manually change the last number to a NaN

You didn't do it right then. Did you scroll all the way to the end of the data in the level data box right after you placed the laser drone? And you can't click out of the box before you hit L, or it won't work either. You'll see the L input into the data and as the level loads it will change itself to NaN. It works for me.

#404860 51 2008-01-28T01:32:03Z Pheidippides
I'm all for this idea. Also, Condog hit it on the nose. All you have to do is find the maximum number of gold pieces you can collect and still complete the level, and base the counter off of that. In other words, the number of possible gold pieces becomes the maximum on the counter. That takes care of unreachable gold and gold that gets you killed, because you'd have to complete the level to count it. And the reward for getting all possible gold is a great idea as well.

#411227 15 2008-02-21T18:49:06Z Pheidippides
I agree with pretty much everything everyone else has said. Do the homework, get involved, and you'll be golden. I wouldn't be able to offer much social advice, seeing as I go to an all-guys high school, but I still agree that it's not always best to conform or try too hard to fit in. Ignore other people's opinions. Be your own person, and your high school years will be great. For me, that meant being a geek/nerd/whatever the word is nowadays. I used to not like that image for me, but now in my junior year I'm totally okay with it, and that's helped me tons. People won't give you crap for being a geek if they see that you're okay with geekdom. I hope you can somehow translate that to your own, probably less geeky experience.

Also, people have mentioned a lot of clubs, but not too many have mentioned sports. Cross Country and Track don't have cuts in most places. I highly suggest joining those teams. I joined track freshman year just because I wanted to do a sport, and now I love running. I'm even running varsity Cross Country and distance Track events. It really is a fantastic sport if you give it time. Cross Country also allows you to meet kids in your class before you even go to your first classes in the fall. You'll have familiar faces from the get-go.

Hope this adds to the great advice you've already gotten. Good luck.

Edit: Eh, I just realized that you probably won't see this, seeing as it's been a week.

#19360 1 2008-02-27T21:18:47Z Pheidippides
Quote: Viridovix, February 27, 2008 12:51 am
Quote: Pheidippides, February 26, 2008 09:41 pm

And about that line in your sig, "two mountains without a valley in between" is called a plateau, or a mesa, or, if they're close enough, one mountain. God can create a plateau, a mesa, or a mountain.
It's just a Gaelic proverb stating that even God is flawed. Or perhaps not capable of everything. You can take it many ways, thus, you don't take it literally.
If God's flawed and/or can't do everything, he's not God. God is perfect and omnipotent. Still, as I'll eventually explain, that claim still does little to resolve the argument; there's not much to argue, or too much.

Quote: Dave, February 27, 2008 11:00 am
If God has created a mesa, a plateau, or a single mountain; then in what way has he created two mountains? I think people have to play with logic or semantics to get certain answers they want.

Things such as plateaus can be seen as two mountains without a valley in between. And if you think of a mountain as a plateau, which can be seen as two mountains, that's so squished in on itself that it isn't flat on top, then that's two mountains, still with no valley. Nevertheless, Dave's right. I was just twisting logic around to get the answer I wanted, but to me the "answer" seemed obvious enough to warrant an argument.

Humans have devised many sayings such as this proverb in an attempt to sort of outsmart God. Whether playing with logic or semantics can "solve" these sayings with a tentative argument such as the one that I used here or not, it ultimately comes down to faith. If you believe in God, you believe that because He is God He knows things that we don't, like how to make a rock he can't lift or what have you. The person who thought up that proverb obviously did not feel this way, as the proverb technically denies God's existence as God. Still, the fact that it presents an impossible task before God does not prove anything, nor does the fact that I barely, if at all, managed to think it possible. It's a moot point. You could argue semantics forever, but in the end it's a matter of faith. My faith tells me that Vir's proverb is nonsense, and so I tried to point out why I think it's nonsensical, but his opinion is still just as valid as mine and I can't disprove it completely, as no one has all the answers about God. To each his own. Agree to disagree.

Sorry, Vir and Dave, I can't do much better than that in terms of argument. I really am a terrible debater.

Back on-topic, the pledge also comes down to what you believe. If you believe that we are a nation under God, and the majority of the nation does, then say "under God". If you don't believe that, don't say the line. Sorry, atheists and non-Christians, but majority rules. The pledge not only shouldn't, but won't change. I stand by Pascal's Wager and the fact that, quite frankly, not everything has to be politically correct down to the punctuation. Epigone points out that "God" could be the God of more than one religion, and if that isn't good enough, why should we go out of our way to accommodate absolutely everyone? Next thing you know, people will start protesting about the lack of political correctness in all of the people on U.S. bills and coins being white. (That is, except for Sacagawea on the dollar coin, which has since been nearly abandoned anyway. What's more, she's practically been replaced by the U.S. Presidents on the golden dollar, according to Wikipedia.)

I'm also leaving because there's no more "magic", if you will. When I joined here, everyone was more happy and enthusiastic about things. It was like I was in a world where I was accepted. The "new-age" people aren't what the people back then were like. The respected members seem to give off that "I'm cool and awesome, but I'm modest and humble" attitude. I might be insecure here, but I feel that whenever I do something I'm proud of, there's something that brings me down. The point is, this place isn't doing it for me anymore. I was hoping that I could stay here until I was 13, and that's not very far away. But I need to move on.

Bye, guys. I really can't waste my time here.

Gah. See ya, Zres. Good luck.

The All-Metanet Track Team

I've realized after reading this thread that we've got quite a few runners on this forum, so I thought it would be neat to have a thread where everyone can post their personal bests. Maybe it'll spark some friendly competition. We'll do this DyeStat style. Post your personal bests for the events that you run, and I'll update the lists. If you want any events added, just say so and I'll add 'em on.

Note: I realize that the 1600 poses a problem because many people run the 1500 or full mile. For the purposes of this thread, everyone will convert their times to the 1600. Use this calculator to convert times. Just select the distance, punch in your time, and check the 1600 result in the 'Purdy' column. Use that time here, and be sure to note which event you've converted your time from.

Edit: Added jumping events and pole vault. Also, Grassman 7z7 brings up a good point here about calculating your times. Please note that the calculator linked above is only for use in converting times between the 1500, 1600, and full mile because some places run things differently than others. In other words, you can't project a 400m time from your 100m or 200m time.

Quote: Grassman7z7, 2008-05-19T14:20:14Z
theres that whole factor about getting tired as you go farther.
QFE. Even though you used the calculator, there's still no way to guarantee you'd be able to run those times, especially based on a 40yd time. It's like when Pokemon are hatched at level 1 and their stats are all pretty much the same; they grow into Pokemon with totally unique stats anyway. I know it's a bad example, but you could run a fast 40 and not have the endurance to run a really quick 200 or 400. I know several guys on the track team that are like rockets in the really short races, but they're not as phenomenal in the 200 on up. You could also run a fast 40 and be an exceptional athlete who can run that fast even for 400m. That's why the calculator is only for the necessity of translating 1500/1600/full mile times. I mean, with my 1600 time, I should run a 100m in 11.6 based on the calculator, and I /know/ that I'll never be that quick.

#20514 1 2008-06-13T20:46:49Z Pheidippides
Ok, so I've had this idea for about a year now, and I tried presenting it to the higher powers (e.g. Blizz) a long time ago, but it never got anywhere. It's a massive contest and sort of an RPG at the same time, but has the potential (I think) to be really fun, considering it can be run as long as people are still interested, has no limits on participation, and can even be joined at any time. Wanna hear it? Ok, here it goes.

You are a Neditor. You have recently begun your quest to become the most skilled of all Neditors, and have journeyed to Neditor Nation. Here, you compete against other Neditors by making maps. To assert your supremacy in Neditor Nation, you must out-map some of the best Neditors around. It wont be easy, either. The road to becoming Champion is a long and rigorous one are you ready?

So, think Pokmon. The Neditors you have to out-map are the eight Gym Leaders, plus the Elite Four. In defeating the Elite Four, a Neditor becomes Champion, and defends his title against those who defeat the Elite Four after him. If he loses to such a challenger, the challenger succeeds him as Champion and the process continues. Thats the great thing about the contest. You can jump in anytime you like, take as long as you want (if youre not in the race to become the first Champion), and the contest can be ended whenever it is necessary. Now Ill go into the rules:

Rules:
All Neditors would have to sign up in a specific thread. This is so I could add them to an Excel Worksheet, which I would use to monitor the progress of the entire Neditor Nation. Its an easy way to prevent Neditors from cheating by trying to skip Gyms and whatnot. Speaking of which, a Gym challenge would work like this:
  • A Gym has been set up with its Leader before the start of the contest (In its own thread? I dont know.). Each of the eight Gyms represents a different N enemy: gauss, rockets, mines, etc. The Gym Leader will make a map prior to the start of the contest that fits two specific guidelines:
    -- The map must only use the enemy specified by the Gym, and
    -- Another specification chosen by the gym-leader, i.e. jump-only, e-tiles only, etc.
  • A Judge would score this map before the start of the contest. The map and score then serve as a standard that Neditors will be trying to exceed in the contest. To do so, a Neditor must make a map that meets the two requirements of the Gym (the enemy choice and the other, unique specification used in the Gym Leaders map) and outscore the Leaders map.
  • If the challenger is not successful, they can try again. However, a challenger cannot post a new map until his last one has been scored by the Judge.
If the challenger is successful, he has cleared the Gym and is able to move on to the next one. After clearing all eight Gyms, a Neditor will challenge the Elite Four. In this challenge, the members of the Elite Four agree upon any two specifications (There need not be an enemy choice, but there could be.), and together they make a map using these two specifications to serve as the standard for their challenge. A Judge would then score it, and Neditors would challenge the Elite Four in the same way as they would a Gym Leader, by making a map to exceed the standard set by the challenge. This leads me to scoring
  • A rubric for ALL maps would need to be agreed upon. It should probably be something simple, like an overall scale of 0 to 20.
  • We would need many judges for this to work to cover up for absences. Before the start of the contest, all judges would need to score all of the Gym Leaders maps and the Elite Four map. That way, any Judge can score a challenger in any Gym. This helps keep the ball rolling.
  • Each time a new Champion is crowned, all the Judges would have to judge the Champions map. This leads me to the job of the Champion
The Champion is an ordinary Neditor who has successfully completed all eight Gyms and has surpassed the Elite Four. The first Champion attains his position with no further Challenges after the Elite Four. After the first Champion is crowned, he must defend his title against other prospective Champions (other Neditors who have defeated the Elite Four). To do so, he makes a map with two specifications, like the Elite Four does prior to the start of the competition. The Judges all score this map, and other Neditors can then challenge the Champion by making a map that meets his specifications. If, when the Judge scores the challengers map, it scores higher than the map of the Champion, the challenger becomes the new Champion, and the old Champion is eliminated from the contest permanently.

Heres an example to explain the game, as I understand its very complicated
  • Neditor challenges Gym Leader. Neditor makes a map encompassing the two specifications set by the Gym Leader. Judge then scores Neditors map, compares that score with HIS OWN score for the Gym Leaders map, and determines the winner. If Neditor succeeds, he advances to the next Gym.
  • If Neditor surpasses all eight Gyms in such a manner, Neditor challenges Elite Four. Neditor makes a map encompassing the Elite Fours two specifications. Judge then scores Neditors map, compares that score with the score HE GAVE the Elite Fours map, and determines the winner. If Neditor succeeds and is the first to surpass the Elite Four, he becomes Champion uncontested.
  • If Neditor is not the first Champion, Neditor must challenge the reigning Champion for his position. Upon becoming Champion, the Champion set two specifications, made a map encompassing those specifications, and had it scored, setting the standard. Neditor, upon challenging the Champion, makes a map encompassing the Champions specifications and has his map scored against the standard. If Neditor is successful, he is the new Champion, and the old Champion may no longer participate in the League (having achieved everything already). If unsuccessful, Neditor may try again.
  • Again, a challenger may not post a map to retry a Gym or other challenge until his first map is scored by a Judge. This keeps the Judges from becoming hopelessly backed up with challengers maps.
  • While this whole proceeding is going on, I will keep tabs on every Neditor using an Excel Worksheet. This prevents cheating and makes it easy to hand out prizes when the contest is ended.
Did somebody say Prizes? (Need a go-ahead from the global mods for some and help from imagers for others)
  • For the First Champion: Custom Title and a Champion Sig Tag
  • For the Longest-Reigning Champion (Champion with the most wins against challengers before being defeated): Custom Title and a Champion Sig Tag
  • For the other Champions: Champion Sig Tags
  • For everyone else: A sig tag showing how many Gyms they cleared while competing in the contest.
  • The Gym Leaders, Elite Four, and Judges should get a little something too, methinks.
Other Ideas:
  • Scrimmages? Neditors would challenge each other for fun, setting a specification each. The two specifications would then have to be encompassed in both Neditors maps, and a third Neditor would judge the maps. Records could be compiled, and it would add another dimension, such as player rankings, to the contest.
  • Gym Leaders, the Elite Four, Judges, and myself could swap the judging roles around to allow for everyone to join in on the game
A few notes:
  • In my opinion, Gym Leaders should represent the best mappers out there. Therefore, they will be selected and not volunteered. It's a lot more epic if you topple mappers like atob, blue_tetris, and the other greats to become Champion, isn't it? Note that all the Gym Leaders do is have to make one map, so hopefully we can still see participation from the veterans who are a bit less active than the rest of us. Judges and I will handle the bulk of the work.
  • Speaking of which, we need a lot of judges to make this work. That's where the rest of the community comes in. If we get enough, the Judges should be able to rotate duties to challenge the Gyms themselves.
  • I'm going to get some concept art up on Imageshack to give any helpful imagers a place to start. My work is mostly done in MS Paint and is therefore rather subpar.
Any questions? I tried my best to put this rather complex idea into words, but there are probably places where I fail to get my point across and details I forgot to mention. Feel free to post with any questions or ideas. Feedback is really what I'm looking for here, not sign-ups or anything. I'd love to get this going because it'd be something that could last all summer and beyond, but I'll need your feedback and your help.

Thanks,

Pheidi

#445143 60 2008-06-16T01:21:29Z Pheidippides
Wow, a lot of questions. I'll try to answer as best as I can. Here we go:

The Gym Leaders keep the same maps the whole time and are in a set order. Palemoon's example there with Charmander is spot on.

When a Neditor challenges a Gym, the Judge compares the score he gave the Neditor with the score he gave the leader. For example, if the Judge gave the Leader a 17/20, and gave the Neditor a 15/20, the Neditor loses.

The Elite Four depends on how many leaders we get. If we get enough, it would be a four-way collab map. If not, it could be a sort of ninth Gym Leader, with one mapper. Yes, the four-way would be tough to beat, but the +2 AGD bonus (great idea, palemoon) will help alleviate the difficulty to a beatable level.

A map is judged on overall quality on a scale of 1 to 20.

About scrimmaging, I was thinking of an "overall score" sort of feature to rank the top 10 or 20 or however many players. Scrimmage wins and losses would help/hurt your overall score, but wouldn't impede your progress in the game. I was thinking of using an equation. I'm just spitballing here, but maybe something like: (Gyms completed x 100) + (Scrimmage wins x 10) - (Scrimmage losses x 10) + (Gym AGDs x 10). That could use refining, but I like the idea.

Okay, Gym Leaders. If no one else from Legacy responds, I am asking:
  • Evil_Bob,
  • Yahoozy,
  • Palemoon,
  • rocket_thumped,
  • lord_day,
  • and Mirage members
if they want in. After that, I guess it's up to nomination. If you think I've missed any important mappers, please say so.

My one problem with that suggestion is: then who is Champion, if the Champion steps down to take up another role? I am going to let the Gym Leaders participate for fun.
