You forget that their Gym requires no input from them after it is set up. Once the Gym is set up, the Leader can basically do whatever he wants. Like I said before, the Judges and I have the biggest problem, keeping track of it all. So the Gym Leaders could jump in, if they want. I'm still debating whether to let them skip their own Gym or requiring that they outmap it. Still, a Leader could not become Champion because they would then have two roles at once and would be interfering with prizes.

As for Judges participating, it depends on how many we get. If we get enough, they could swap in and out the entire time because there would be enough scores to where there would always be a Judge that wasn't them to score a Gym challenge. Maybe, a Judge gets a week off-duty to challenge the Gyms, then another Judge gets his chance the next week, and when the first Judge gets a turn again, he picks up where he left off the last time. I was also thinking of having the dethroned Champions become Judges to make sure those ranks are adequately filled. They'd be out of the game otherwise, so that would be an opportunity for them to keep participating.

If I'm confused anyone by my rambling, just ask a question and I can clarify. I think.

Are there any other questions?

Oh yeah, and I forgot to include AGDs in the Player Score formula. Someone argued that scrimmage wins aren't as important, and I agree, so I'm going to try again.

Player Score = [(Gyms Completed + Wins in becoming/after becoming Champion)/(Gym Attempts + Champion Attempts + Times Defending Championship)] x 10000 + [Scrimmage Wins/Total Scrimmages] x 1000 + [Gym/Champion AGDs] x 100 + Special Event Bonuses.

What do you think? Whew, another very long post.

Edit: Whoops, forgot to mention that if you'd like to start scrimmaging, then go right ahead, but they won't count for anything until the contest starts. Consider them warmups.
#448890 177 2008-06-26T18:03:26Z Pheidippides
Yeah, I do like lord_day's idea. I was overcomplicating things with that equation.

#453486 211 2008-07-18T00:45:29Z Pheidippides
Quote: lord_day, July 17, 2008 06:52 pm
Well maybe people can send their challenges at any time, but judges only start judging every Monday?

The thing is if the Gyms are open all week, there's a possibility that the Judges could be inundated with challenges. This applies especially at the start of the contest, when everyone will be challenging the first Gym pretty much right away. To solve that, I suggest 3 days of map submissions, followed by 4 days of Judging. This also should ensure that each challenger is given the result of his challenge by the end of the week, instead of having to wait for the undetermined, arbitrary amount of time it takes to Judge the map. Judging could even start as soon as submissions are received, not necessarily on Wednesday. I just want to be sure that the contest doesn't become clogged, because that would effectively kill it.

#465218 19 2008-08-28T22:15:36Z Pheidippides
I've had the album for a few weeks now, and I must say, it's /very/ good. Life in Technicolor provided a last bit of hype before I got to the meat of the album; the escalating music really got me excited to hear the other 9 songs. Cemeteries of London is a great, entertainingly dark-ish song with a great beat. Lost! and 42 were pretty good, but the album really gets good beginning with Lovers in Japan. Love that song. Yes is another good one, followed by Viva La Vida which, as good as it is, actually doesn't stand out because the supporting songs are all so great. Violet Hill is fantastic, a little harder rock that finishes with a somber little piano bit that provides a good contrast to bring it home. Strawberry Swing is a great little number, and Death and All His Friends ties it up very nicely just as the album began. All in all, I'm very glad I bought this. 9.9/10.

Pretty. Odd. by Panic at the Disco was good as well. Bought it on the same day as Viva La Vida, no less. I was in good-song overload for a bit over a week.
